for all
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},{{g}}\in\Gamma
.

Notation
.

For
\bar{\beta}\in H^{3}(Q\otimes_{K}\overline{K},\mu_{n})^{\Gamma_{K}}
and
x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\in E^{*}
, we let

R_{\bar{\beta}}[x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}]_{n}\coloneqq\sum_{[(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3})]\in\Gamma^{3}/\Gamma}a_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}}(\bar{\beta})[x_{1}^{\sigma_{1}},x_{2}^{\sigma_{2}},x_{3}^{\sigma_{3}}]_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z},

when all the Redèi symbols appearing on the right hand side are defined.

The indexing in the last sum is well-posed as

a_{\sigma_{1}{{g}},\sigma_{2}{{g}},\sigma_{3}{{g}}}(\bar{\beta})[x_{1}^{\sigma_{1}{{g}}},x_{2}^{\sigma_{2}{{g}}},x_{3}^{\sigma_{3}{{g}}}]_{n}=\\
(a_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}}(\bar{\beta})[x_{1}^{\sigma_{1}},x_{2}^{\sigma_{2}},x_{3}^{\sigma_{3}}]_{n})^{{{g}}}=a_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}}(\bar{\beta})[x_{1}^{\sigma_{1}},x_{2}^{\sigma_{2}},x_{3}^{\sigma_{3}}]_{n}

for all
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},{{g}}\in\Gamma
, where in the first identity we used (
12.2
) and
\bar{\beta}^{{{g}}}=\bar{\beta}
, and in the second we used that each summand
a_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}}(\bar{\beta})[x_{1}^{\sigma_{1}},x_{2}^{\sigma_{2}},x_{3}^{\sigma_{3}}]_{n}\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})(-2)\otimes(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})(2)=\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
is Galois-invariant.

Theorem 12.1

(Triple variation)
.

Let
f:X\rightarrow Q
be a smooth projective fibration over a Galois quasi-trivial torus
Q=R_{E/K}\mathbb{G}_{m}
with a multi-section
\bar{s}:Q^{\overline{K},n,(1)}\rightarrow X
for some
n
such that
\mu_{n}\subset E
. There exists then a finite Galois extension
L/K,\,L\subset\overline{K}
such that
\bar{s}=s\circ(Q^{\overline{K},n,(1)}\rightarrow Q^{L,n,(1)})
for some
s:Q^{L,n,(1)}\rightarrow X
and for which, for all sufficiently large finite sets of places
S
of
K
, the following holds.

For all
(P_{v})_{v\in S}\in X(K_{S})
, all
n
-torsion elements
b\in\Br_{\text{hor}}(X/Q)
, all sufficiently small neighbourhoods
U_{v}
of
P_{v}
for non-archimedean
v\in S
, all arbitrarily small neighbourhood
U_{\infty}
of the image of
P_{\infty}=(P_{v})_{v\in M_{K}^{\infty}}\in X(K_{\infty})
in
X(K_{\infty})_{dir}
,
any
(L,n)
-liftable prime-variational cube

\mathcal{M}:=q_{0}\cdot\{q_{1,1},q_{1,2}\}\cdot\{q_{2,1},q_{2,2}\}\cdot\{q_{3,1},q_{3,2}\}\subset\mathcal{O}_{E,S}\subset E^{*}=Q(K)

that is contained in
f(U_{v})
for all finite
v\in S
, and whose image in
Q(K_{\infty})_{dir}
is contained in
f(U_{\infty})
, satifies

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q=q_{0}q_{1}^{i}q_{2}^{j}q_{3}^{l}\\
0\leq i,j,l\leq 1\end{subarray}}(-1)^{i+j+l}\cdot(b,(P_{v}(q))_{v\in M_{K}})_{BM}=R_{{\bar{\partial}(b)}}[\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3}]_{n},

where
(P_{v}(q))_{v\in M_{K}}\in X_{q}(\mathbb{A}_{K})
is a compatible choice of adelic lifts constructed through
s
as in Section
11
, and
\delta_{i}:=q_{i,2}q_{i,1}^{-1}
.

The rest of this section is devoted to proving Theorem
12.1
through an explicit computation with Čech cochains.

12.2.
Vertical Brauer–Manin pairing via Čech cohomology

In this subsection, we describe how to compute the Brauer–Manin pairing attached to a horizontal Brauer element using Čech cocycles. In the computation, it will be more convenient to work with inverse systems of coverings rather than with a single covering. For that reason, we operate with what we call “procoverings” (pro-objects in the category of coverings of a scheme). It is straightforward that such inverse systems, just as usual Čech coverings, give rise to Čech complexes that can be used to approximate the cohomology of schemes, and to compute it exactly for quasi-projective varieties. Mainly just to settle notation, we briefly pause to lay out these properties. Afterwards, we shift our attention to relative Čech cohomology and relative cohomology, summarizing the content of Appendix
B
. We then conclude this subsection with an explicit formula for the vertical Brauer–Manin pairing.

Procoverings and procovered schemes.
A
procovering
of a scheme
Y
is an object of the pro-category of coverings of
Y
(i.e. the category of inverse systems of coverings of
Y
), and a refinement of procoverings is a morphism in this pro-category. A
procovered scheme
is a pair
(Y,\mathcal{U})
of a scheme
Y
and a procovering
\mathcal{U}/Y
. A morphism between procovered schemes
(X,\mathcal{V})
and
(Y,\mathcal{U})
is a pair
(f,\psi)
of a morphism
f:X\rightarrow Y
and a refinement
\psi:\mathcal{V}\rightarrow\mathcal{U}\times_{Y}X
.

If
Y^{\prime}\rightarrow Y
is a morphism of schemes with
Y^{\prime}
an inverse limit of finite étale covers of
Y
, we denote by
\{Y^{\prime}/Y\}
its associated procovering and by
(Y,Y^{\prime})
the associated procovered scheme.

Pro-Čech-complex and pro-Čech-to-étale maps.
Recall that to an étale covering
\mathcal{U}
of a scheme
Y
and a sheaf
F
on
Y
, we may associate a Čech complex
\check{C}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{U},F)
, and that there are natural maps

(12.3)

\check{H}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{U},F)\rightarrow H^{\bullet}(Y,F)

from its cohomology to that of
Y

[
34
, §III.2]
. For a procovering
\mathcal{U}
, we define
\check{C}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{U},F):=\lim\limits_{\rightarrow}\check{C}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{U}_{i},F)
, where
\mathcal{U}_{i},\,i\in I
is an inverse system of coverings with inverse limit
\mathcal{U}
. The map (
12.3
) extends naturally to procoverings.

Specialization to points.

For a field
\Omega
and a covering
\mathcal{U}=\{U_{i}\rightarrow Y\}_{i\in I}
of a scheme
Y
, we let

\mathcal{U}(\Omega):=\bigcup_{i}U_{i}(\Omega).

For a procovering
\mathcal{U}=\lim\limits_{\leftarrow}\mathcal{U}_{i}
, we let
\mathcal{U}(\Omega):=\lim\limits_{\leftarrow}\mathcal{U}_{i}(\Omega)
.

If
y\in Y(\Omega)
is an
\Omega
-point, then each point
\bar{y}\in\mathcal{U}(\Omega^{\text{sep}})
whose image in
Y
is (the geometric point corresponding to)
y
gives rise to a morphism of procovered schemes

(y,\bar{y}):(\Spec\Omega,\Spec\Omega^{\text{sep}})\rightarrow(Y,\mathcal{U}),

inducing pullback maps

(12.4)

\check{C}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{U}/Y,F)\rightarrow\check{C}^{\bullet}(\Omega^{\text{sep}}/\Omega,F|_{y}),

on Čech cochains for any étale sheaf
F
on
Y
. We denote the image of a cochain
\alpha
under this map by
\alpha(y,\bar{y})
.

A special example is when
\mathcal{U}/Y
is comprised of a single Galois covering
Y^{\prime}\rightarrow Y
, say with group
G
. In this case, (
12.4
) becomes

(12.5)

\check{C}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{U}/Y,F)\rightarrow\check{C}^{\bullet}(\Omega^{\text{sep}}/\Omega,F|_{y}),

under the identifications
\check{C}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{U}/Y,F)=C^{\bullet}(G,F(Y^{\prime}))
and
\check{C}^{\bullet}(\Omega^{\text{sep}}/\Omega,F|_{y})=\linebreak{C}^{\bullet}(\Gal(\Omega^{sep}/\Omega),F|_{y}(\bar{y}))
given by (
2.3
).

Relative étale cohomology.
For a morphism of schemes
f:X\rightarrow Y
, we denote by
\Sh_{X/Y}
the category of triples
(F,G,\alpha:F\rightarrow f_{*}G)
, where
F
(resp.
G
) is a sheaf on
Y
(resp.
X
). We call such a triple a
relative sheaf
. We define the
relative étale cohomology groups

H^{\bullet}_{X}(Y;M)
of a relative sheaf
M=(F,G,\alpha)
as the derived functors of

\Gamma_{X}(Y,-):\Sh_{X/Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}b:(F,G,\alpha)\mapsto\Ker(\alpha:F(Y)\rightarrow G(X)).

See Appendix
B
for details. (See also
[
12
, Ch. 14]
for an alternative equivalent definition via simplicial schemes). These groups sit in a natural long exact sequence

(12.6)

\cdots H^{n}(Y,F)\xrightarrow{\alpha^{\dagger}\circ f^{*}}H^{n}(X,G)\rightarrow H_{X}^{n+1}(Y,M)\rightarrow H^{n+1}(Y,F)\rightarrow\cdots.

Relative Čech-to-étale comparison.
A
relative covering
of
f
is a triple
(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V},\psi:\mathcal{V}\rightarrow\mathcal{U}\times_{Y}X)
, where
\mathcal{U}
(resp.
\mathcal{V}
) is a covering of
Y
(resp.
X
), and
\psi
is a refinement. For a relative covering and a relative étale sheaf
(F,G,\alpha)
, the
relative Čech complex

\check{C}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{V}/X,\mathcal{U}/Y;F,G)
is the mapping cone of the map of complexes
\check{C}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{U}/Y,F)\rightarrow\check{C}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{V}/X,G)
induced by pullback along
f
and the left adjunct
\alpha^{\dagger}:f^{-1}F\rightarrow G
of
\alpha
.
The cohomology of this cone sits in a long exact sequence

(12.7)

\cdots\rightarrow\check{H}^{n}(\mathcal{U}/Y,F)\rightarrow\check{H}^{n}(\mathcal{V}/X,G)\rightarrow\check{H}^{n+1}(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{U};F,G)\rightarrow\check{H}^{n+1}(\mathcal{U}/Y,F)\rightarrow\cdots.

There are natural maps

(12.8)

\check{H}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{V}/X,\mathcal{U}/Y;F,G)\rightarrow H^{\bullet}_{X}(Y;F,G)

from
relative Čech cohomology
to
relative étale cohomology
. See Appendix
B
for the details on their construction. These maps are compatible with (
12.6
) and (
12.7
).

A
relative procovering
of
f:X\rightarrow Y
is a pro-object in the category of relative coverings of
f
. Analogously as for (
12.3
), the maps (
12.8
) also naturally extend to relative procoverings of
f
.

Vertical Brauer–Manin pairing.
Let
f:X\rightarrow Y
be a smooth proper morphism with geometrically integral fibers. We denote by
\mathbb{G}_{m}
denote the relative sheaf
(\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathbb{G}_{m},\text{id})
. We then have an identification (see (
B.3
))

{H}^{3}(X,Y;\mathbb{G}_{m})=\Br_{\text{hor}}(X/Y).

The following lemma gives the formula computing the vertical Brauer–Manin pairing. Its proof is completely straightforward, and mostly just a matter of unraveling the notation involved.

Assume here that
K
is a number field.

Lemma 12.2
.

Let
(\mathcal{U},\psi:\mathcal{V}\rightarrow\mathcal{U}\times_{Y}X)
be a relative procovering of
f
with
\psi
surjective. Let
\overline{K}\subset\overline{K}_{v}
be given embeddings for each
v
, and let
y\in Y(K),(P_{v})_{v}\in X_{y}(\mathbb{A}_{K})
,
\bar{y}\in\mathcal{U}(\overline{K}),\bar{P}_{v}\in\mathcal{V}(\overline{K}_{v})
for each
v
be such that the diagram of procovered schemes

(12.9)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Spec K_{v},\Spec\overline{K}_{v})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(X,\mathcal{V})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\Spec K,\Spec\overline{K})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(Y,\mathcal{U})}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(P_{v},\bar{P}_{v})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f,\psi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(y,\bar{y})}

commutes. Then for any
\check{b}\in\check{H}^{2}(\mathcal{V}/X,\mathcal{U}/Y;\mathbb{G}_{m})
, any representative
u=(\alpha,\beta)\in\check{Z}^{2}(\mathcal{V}/X,\mathcal{U}/Y;\mathbb{G}_{m})
of
\check{b}
, and any
\gamma\in C^{2}(\overline{K}/K,\mathbb{G}_{m})
such that

\d\gamma=\beta(y,\bar{y}),

we have

(12.10)

((P_{v})_{v},b)_{BM}=\sum_{v}\inv_{v}\left(\alpha_{v}-\gamma_{v}\right),

where
\alpha_{v}:=\alpha(P_{v},\bar{P}_{v}),\ \gamma_{v}:=\gamma|_{\Gamma_{v}}
, and
b
denotes the image of
\check{b}
under the map

\check{H}^{2}(\mathcal{V}/X,\mathcal{U}/Y;\mathbb{G}_{m})\rightarrow H^{2}(X/Y,\mathbb{G}_{m})=\Br_{\text{hor}}(X/Y).

Remark 12.3
.

(1). We note that a
\gamma
as in the lemma always exists, as
\beta(y,\bar{y})
lies in
\check{Z}^{3}(\overline{K}/K,\mathbb{G}_{m})
, and
\check{H}^{3}(\overline{K}/K,\mathbb{G}_{m})={H}^{3}(\overline{K}/K,\mathbb{G}_{m})=0
since
K
is a number field
[
5
, VII.11.4, p. 199]
.

(2). Given a relative procovering with
\psi
surjective, and points
y\in Y(K),(P_{v})\in X_{y}(\mathbb{A}_{K})
, one may always find lifts
\bar{y},\overline{P_{v}(q)}
satisfying the commutativity (
12.9
) as follows.

First, given any covering
\mathcal{U}/Y
and a point
y\in Y(K)
, we may always find a lift
\bar{y}\in\mathcal{U}(\overline{K})
lying above it using the surjectivity of
\mathcal{U}\rightarrow Y
.
Then, given any relative covering
(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V};\psi:\mathcal{V}\rightarrow\mathcal{U}\times_{Y}X)
with
\psi
surjective, points
y\in Y(K),(P_{v})\in X_{y}(\mathbb{A}_{K})
, and a lift
\bar{y}\in\mathcal{U}(\overline{K})
of
y
, letting
\overline{P_{v}}\in\mathcal{V}(\overline{K}_{v})
be any inverse image of the pair
(\bar{y}\otimes_{\overline{K}}\overline{K}_{v},P_{v}\otimes_{K_{v}}\overline{K}_{v})\in(\mathcal{U}\times_{Y}X)(\overline{K}_{v})\subset\mathcal{U}(\overline{K}_{v})\times X(\overline{K}_{v})
under the surjective map
\mathcal{V}(\overline{K}_{v})\rightarrow(\mathcal{U}\times_{Y}X)(\overline{K}_{v})
, diagram (
12.9
) commutes automatically.

Proof.

After pulling back all the objects involved along
y\hookrightarrow Y
, we may assume that
Y=\Spec K
. Morover, we may assume that
(\mathcal{U},\psi:\mathcal{V}\rightarrow\mathcal{U}\times_{Y}X)
is a relative covering.

We first prove that the right hand side of (
12.10
) does not depend on the choices of
u
and
\gamma
. The independence on
\gamma
follows immediately from the Albert–Brauer–Hasse–Noether theorem. For the independence on
u
, let
v=(\psi^{*}f^{*}\delta,\d\delta)\in\check{B}^{2}(\mathcal{V}/X,\mathcal{U}/Y;\mathbb{G}_{m})
,
\delta\in\check{C^{2}}(\mathcal{V}/Y,\mathbb{G}_{m})
be a coborder.
For each
v
, Diagram (
12.9
) induces by pullback a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\check{C}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{V}/X,\mathbb{G}_{m})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\check{C}^{\bullet}(\overline{K}_{v}/K_{v},\mathbb{G}_{m})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\check{C}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{U}/Y,\mathbb{G}_{m})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\check{C}^{\bullet}(\overline{K}/K,\mathbb{G}_{m})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha\mapsto\alpha(P_{v},\bar{P}_{v})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi^{*}f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha\mapsto\alpha(y,\bar{y})}

on Čech cochains, where the horizontal maps are
\alpha\mapsto\alpha(P_{v},\bar{P}_{v})
and
\alpha\mapsto\alpha(y,\bar{y})
, respectively. Identifying the image of
\gamma
under the two compositions, we get

(\psi^{*}f^{*})(\delta)(P_{v},\bar{P}_{v})=\delta(y,\bar{y})|_{v}.

So
\alpha^{\prime}_{v}=\alpha_{v}-\delta(y,\bar{y})|_{v}
, and we infer that the right hand side of (
12.10
) remains the same after replacing
(u,\gamma)
by
(u^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}):=(u-v,\gamma-\delta),
showing the independence on
u
.

Finally, to prove formula (
12.10
), let us first prove that
\mathcal{V}_{\bar{y}}:=\mathcal{V}\times_{\mathcal{U}}\bar{y}
is a procovering of
X
via the composition
\mathcal{V}\times_{\mathcal{U}}\bar{y}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}\rightarrow X
. Since it is clearly an inverse system of collections of étale maps over
X
, we only need to show that
\mathcal{V}_{\bar{y}}:=\mathcal{V}\times_{\mathcal{U}}\bar{y}\rightarrow X
is surjective. To do so, note first that the projection

\mathcal{V}_{\bar{y}}:=\mathcal{V}\times_{\mathcal{U}}\bar{y}=\mathcal{V}\times_{(\mathcal{U}\times_{Y}X)}(\bar{y}\times_{Y}X)\rightarrow\bar{y}\times_{Y}X

is surjective because the map
\mathcal{V}\rightarrow\mathcal{U}\times_{Y}X
is. The projection
\bar{y}\times_{Y}X\rightarrow X
is surjective as
\bar{y}\rightarrow Y
is, and so
\mathcal{V}_{\bar{y}}\rightarrow X
is surjective too, proving that
\mathcal{V}_{\bar{y}}
is a procovering of
X
as wished.

We may thus pullback the statement along the refinement of relative procoverings

(\bar{y}/y,\mathcal{V}_{\bar{y}}\rightarrow\bar{y}\times_{Y}X)\rightarrow(\mathcal{U},\psi:\mathcal{V}\rightarrow f^{*}\mathcal{U}),

and in doing so reduce the general case to the case
\mathcal{U}=\{\overline{K}/K\}
.
In this case, we have
\check{H}^{3}(\mathcal{U}/K,\mathbb{G}_{m})=\check{H}^{3}(\overline{K}/K,\mathbb{G}_{m})=0
(as
K
is a number field), and so we may choose a representative
u
for
\check{b}
with
\beta=0
. When
\beta=0
, the cocycle
\alpha
is a Čech cocyle representing a class of the usual Brauer group
\Br X
whose image in
\Br_{\text{hor}}(X/K)=\Br X/\Br K
is
b
. Then we may take
\gamma=0
and formula (
12.10
) is just the usual expression for the Brauer–Manin pairing, where we only explicitated the pullbacks of the Brauer class to the points
P_{v}
via Čech cocycles.
∎

12.3.
Proof of Theorem
12.1

We divide the proof in five main parts (distributed among respective subsubsections):

1.

in the first we find a suitable Čech representative
\check{b}
in a group
{\check{H}^{3}(\mathcal{U}/Q,\mu_{n})}
for
b
with coefficients in
\mu_{n}
, with
\mathcal{U}
the universal pro-finite étale cover of
Q
;

2.

in the second we choose lifts as in Lemma
12.2
, set up the computation, and divide the resulting expression in two parts, containing the contribution from the fibers and the base, respectively;

3.

in the third we show that the first of the two parts above vanishes;

4.

in the fourth we show how the second of the two parts above reduces to a sum of Redéi symbols; in doing so, an important tool is Proposition
12.15
, where we compute the “triple variation” of a suitable representative cocycles in
Z^{3}(\pi_{1}(Q),\mu_{n})
;

5.

the fifth part is dedicated to the proof of Proposition
12.15
.

12.3.1.
Choice of Čech representative

We let
Q^{ur}:=\Spec\overline{K}[(y^{\sigma})^{\pm\frac{1}{\omega}}]_{\sigma\in\Gamma}
be the universal cover of
Q
, and
\bar{Q}:=\Spec\overline{K}[(y^{\sigma})^{\pm 1}]_{\sigma\in\Gamma}=Q\otimes_{K}\overline{K}
.

Proposition 12.4
.

There exists a relative procovering of
f
of the form
(\mathcal{V},Q^{ur},\psi:\mathcal{V}\rightarrow Q^{ur}\times_{Q}X)
with
\psi
surjective, and an element

\displaystyle(\alpha,\beta)\in

\displaystyle\check{Z}^{3}(\mathcal{V}/X,Q^{ur}/Q;\mu_{n})

\displaystyle\subset

\displaystyle\check{C}^{2}(\mathcal{V}/X,\mu_{n})\times\check{Z}^{3}(Q^{ur}/Q,\mu_{n})

that maps to
b
under the composition
\check{Z}^{3}(\mathcal{V}/X,Q^{ur}/Q;\mu_{n})\rightarrow H^{3}(X/Q,\mu_{n})\rightarrow\Br_{\text{hor}}(X/Q)
and such that:

i.

\beta
lies in the image of
\check{Z}^{3}(Q^{\overline{K},n}/Q,\mu_{n})\rightarrow\check{Z}^{3}(Q^{ur}/Q,\mu_{n})
;

ii.

the image of
\beta
under the composition
\check{Z}^{3}(Q^{\overline{K},n}/Q,\mu_{n})\rightarrow\check{H}^{3}(Q^{\overline{K},n}/Q,\mu_{n})\rightarrow\check{H}^{3}(Q^{\overline{K},n}/\bar{Q},\mu_{n})
lies in the
(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})(-2)
-linear span of the cup-products
(y^{\sigma_{1}}\cup y^{\sigma_{2}}\cup y^{\sigma_{3}})_{n},\,\{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}\}\in\binom{\Gamma}{3}
;

iii.

there exists a finite extension
L^{\prime}/L
such that the restriction of
\beta
to
\check{Z}^{3}(Q^{ur}/Q^{L^{\prime},n,(1)},\mu_{n})
is trivial;

We divide the proof of Proposition
12.4
in two parts: existence of
(\mathcal{V},Q^{ur},\psi:\mathcal{V}\rightarrow Q^{ur}\times_{Q}X)
and sought representative
(\alpha,\beta)
, and modification of
(\alpha,\beta)
to satisfy i-iii.

We need the following for the first part.

Lemma 12.5
.

Fix a covering
\mathcal{U}/Q
. The relative coverings of the form
(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V},\psi:\mathcal{V}\rightarrow\mathcal{U}\times_{Q}X)
with
\psi
surjective form a filtered system when ordered by refinement. Moreover, the coverings
\mathcal{V}
appearing in the relative coverings in this poset are cofinal among all coverings of
X
. The analagous statement holds with procoverings instead of coverings.

Proof.

Given relative coverings
(\mathcal{U},\psi_{1}:\mathcal{V}_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{U}\times_{Q}X)
and
(\mathcal{U},\psi_{2}:\mathcal{V}_{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{U}\times_{Q}X)
with
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
surjective, the fibered product
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}:=\mathcal{V}_{1}\times_{\psi_{1},\mathcal{U}\times_{Q}X,\psi_{2}}\mathcal{V}_{2}
is another covering of
X
, and it maps surjectively to
\mathcal{U}\times_{Q}X
. Then the relative covering
(\mathcal{U},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}\rightarrow\mathcal{U}\times_{Q}X)
refines both, proving that the relative coverings in the statement form a filtered system. For the cofinality, note that any covering
\mathcal{V}/X
is refined by
\mathcal{V}\times_{X}\mathcal{U}\times_{Q}X
, which can be extended to the relative covering
(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V}\times_{X}\mathcal{U}\times_{Q}X\rightarrow\mathcal{U}\times_{Q}X)
.

The statement on procoverings follows from that on coverings.
∎

Proof of Proposition
12.4
, existence of
(\alpha,\beta)
.

Applying the hypercohomology functors
H^{\bullet}(Q,-)
to the “truncated Kummer” exact triangle

\tau_{\geq 1}Rf_{*}\mu_{n}\rightarrow\tau_{\geq 1}Rf_{*}\mathbb{G}_{m}\xrightarrow{[n]}\tau_{\geq 1}Rf_{*}\mathbb{G}_{m}\xrightarrow{+1},

we infer that the natural map
H^{2}(Q,\tau_{\geq 1}Rf_{*}\mu_{n})\rightarrow H^{2}(Q,\tau_{\geq 1}Rf_{*}\mathbb{G}_{m})[n]
is surjective. Equivalently (see (
B.3
)), the map
H^{3}(X/Q,\mu_{n})\rightarrow H^{3}(X/Q,\mathbb{G}_{m})[n]
is surjective.

Let
b_{0}\in H^{3}(X/Q,\mu_{n})
be an inverse image of
b
. For any relative procovering
(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V},\psi:\mathcal{V}\rightarrow\mathcal{U}\times_{Q}X)
of
f
, we get a commutative diagram with exact rows by comparing (
B.2
) and (the direct limit of) (
B.5
):

(12.11)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\check{H}^{3}(\mathcal{U}/Q,\mu_{n})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\check{H}^{2}(\mathcal{V}/X,\mu_{n})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\check{H}^{3}(\mathcal{V}/X,\mathcal{U}/Q;\mu_{n})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\check{H}^{3}(\mathcal{U}/Q,\mu_{n})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\check{H}^{3}(\mathcal{V}/X,\mu_{n})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{3}(Q,\mu_{n})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{2}(X,\mu_{n})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{3}(X/Q,\mu_{n})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{3}(Q,\mu_{n})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{3}(X,\mu_{n})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}

Let
Q^{\text{ur}}=\Spec\overline{K}[(y^{\sigma})^{\pm\frac{1}{\omega}}]_{\sigma\in\Gamma}
be the profinite étale universal cover of
Q
. The pro-Čech-to-étale maps
\check{H}^{k}(Q^{\text{ur}}/Q,\mu_{n})\rightarrow H^{k}(Q,\mu_{n})
are isomorphisms for all
k
by Hochschild-Serre’s spectral sequence (or rather, its profinite version
[
34
, Remark III.2.21(b)]
) and the vanishing
H^{k}(Q^{\text{ur}},\mu_{n})=0,\,k\geq 1

[
15
, Corollary 3.3]
.
So the first and fourth vertical maps in (
12.11
) are isomorphisms when
\mathcal{U}:=\{Q^{\text{ur}}/Q\}
.

Taking now the direct limit of diagram (
12.11
) over all relative procoverings as in the lemma (taking such limit is allowed by Lemma
B.8
), the second and fifth vertical maps become isomorphisms by the quasi-projectivity of
X
. Then the middle one is an isomorphism as well by the lemma of five. In particular, there exists a relative procovering
(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V},\psi:\mathcal{V}\rightarrow\mathcal{U}\times_{Q}X)
with
\psi
surjective for which
b
lifts to a Čech class
\check{b}
.
Let

(\alpha,\beta)\in\check{Z}^{2}(\mathcal{V}/X,Q^{ur}/Q;\mu_{n})\subset\check{C}^{2}(\mathcal{V}/X,\mu_{n})\times\check{Z}^{3}(Q^{ur}/Q,\mu_{n})

be a representative for
\check{b}
.
∎

Before proceeding to the next part, we introduce some notation.

Notation
.

We set by convention the
fundamental group
of an intermediate cover between
Q^{ur}
and
Q
, i.e. a profinite étale integral cover
Q^{\prime}\rightarrow Q
sitting in a diagram
Q^{ur}\rightarrow Q^{\prime}\rightarrow Q
, to be
\pi_{1}(Q):=\Gal(Q^{ur}/Q)
.

The tower
Q^{ur}\rightarrow\bar{Q}\rightarrow Q
gives by Galois theory a short exact sequence

1\rightarrow\pi_{1}(\bar{Q})\rightarrow\pi_{1}(Q)\rightarrow\Gamma_{K}\rightarrow 1.

We recall that
\pi_{1}(\bar{Q})=X_{*}(Q)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{}\mathbb{Z}(1)
(the same formula applies for any torus over an algebraically closed field of characterstic
0
).
We also let

G_{n}:=\pi_{1}(Q)/n\bar{\pi}_{1}(\bar{Q})=\Gal(Q^{\overline{K},n}/Q)\text{ and }\bar{G}_{n}:=\pi_{1}(\bar{Q})/n\pi_{1}(\bar{Q})=\Gal(Q^{\overline{K},n}/\bar{Q}).

Note that
\bar{G}_{n}=X_{*}(Q)\otimes_{\widehat{}\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})(1)\cong(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})(1)[\Gamma]
. More explicitly, the last isomorphism is given by:

\bar{G}_{n}=\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Gamma}\bar{G}_{n,\sigma},\ \text{ with }\bar{G}_{n,\sigma}:=\Gal(Q^{\overline{K},n}/Q^{\overline{K},n,(\sigma)}),

Q^{\overline{K},n,(\sigma)}:=\Spec\overline{K}[(y^{\sigma^{\prime}})^{\pm\frac{1}{n}},(y^{\sigma})^{\pm 1}]_{\sigma^{\prime}\in\Gamma\setminus\{\sigma\}}.

Note that
\bar{G}_{n,\sigma}\cong\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
as an abstract group.

Lemma 12.6
.

Let
d:=\#\Gamma
and
\sigma_{1}:=\text{id},\sigma_{2},\ldots,\sigma_{d}
be the elements of
\Gamma
. We have a natural decomposition

H^{k}(\bar{G}_{n},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})=\bigoplus_{k_{1}+\cdots+k_{d}=k}\left(H^{k_{1}}(\bar{G}_{n,\sigma_{1}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}\cdots\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}H^{k_{d}}(\bar{G}_{n,\sigma_{d}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\right)

given by the cross-product map from the right to the left hand side.

Proof.

This is just Künneth’s formula
[
49
, Theorem 3.6.3]
applied to the direct sum
\bar{G}_{n}=\bar{G}_{n,\sigma_{1}}\oplus\cdots\oplus\bar{G}_{n,\sigma_{d}}
with coefficients in
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. The flatness hypothesis of Künneth’s formula holds because each
G_{n,\sigma_{i}}
is isomorphic as a group to
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
and
H^{p}(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\cong\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
for every
p\geq 0
.
∎

We define

H^{k}(\bar{G}_{n},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})_{cup}\subset H^{k}(\bar{G}_{n},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})
as the subgroup corresponding, in the Künneth decomposition above, to strings
(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})
where each
k_{i}
is either
0
or
1
.

We shall need the following two lemmas to continue the proof of Proposition
12.4
.

Lemma 12.7
.

The following hold for all
k\geq 0
.

i.

The restriction of the inflation map
H^{k}(\bar{G}_{n},\mu_{n})\rightarrow H^{k}(\pi_{1}(\bar{Q}),\mu_{n})
to

H^{k}(\bar{G}_{n},\mu_{n})\supset H^{k}(\bar{G}_{n},\mu_{n})_{cup}:=H^{k}(\bar{G}_{n},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})_{cup}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{n}

is an isomorphism.

ii.

The Čech-to-étale map
H^{k}(G_{n},\mu_{n})\rightarrow H^{k}(Q,\mu_{n})
is surjective.

iii.

Every element in
H^{k}(Q,\mu_{n})
admits an inverse image in
H^{k}(G_{n},\mu_{n})
whose restriction to
H^{k}(\bar{G}_{n},\mu_{n})
lies in
H^{k}(\bar{G}_{n},\mu_{n})_{cup}
.

Proof of i.

After Tate-twisting, we may prove that the restriction of
\inf:\linebreak H^{k}(\bar{G}_{n},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\rightarrow H^{k}(\pi_{1}(\bar{Q}),\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})
to
H^{k}(\bar{G}_{n},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})_{cup}
is an isomorphism. Let
\pi_{1}(Q)=\oplus_{\sigma\in\Gamma}\bar{G}_{\infty,\sigma}
be a direct sum decomposition lifting the decomposition
\bar{G}_{n}=\oplus_{\sigma\in\Gamma}\bar{G}_{\infty,\sigma}
with each
\bar{G}_{\infty,\sigma}\cong\widehat{}\mathbb{Z}
(e.g. take
\bar{G}_{\infty,\sigma}:=\Gal(Q^{ur}/Q^{\overline{K},\infty,\sigma})
where
Q^{\overline{K},\infty,(\sigma)}:=\Spec\overline{K}[(y^{\sigma^{\prime}})^{\pm\frac{1}{\omega}},(y^{\sigma})^{\pm 1}]_{\sigma^{\prime}\in\Gamma\setminus\{\sigma\}}
). The inflation map
\inf:H^{k}(\bar{G}_{n},\mu_{n})\rightarrow H^{k}(\pi_{1}(\bar{Q}),\mu_{n})
respects the Künneth decompositions associated to the direct sum decompositions, and so we get a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{k}(\bar{G}_{n},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bigoplus_{k_{1}+\cdots+k_{d}=k}\left(H^{k_{1}}(\bar{G}_{n,\sigma_{1}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}\cdots\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}H^{k_{d}}(\bar{G}_{n,\sigma_{d}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{k}(\pi_{1}(\bar{Q}),\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bigoplus_{k_{1}+\cdots+k_{d}=k}\left(H^{k_{1}}(\bar{G}_{\infty,\sigma_{1}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}\cdots\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}H^{k_{d}}(\bar{G}_{\infty,\sigma_{d}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\right)}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\inf}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\inf}

The natural inflation map
\inf:H^{k}(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\rightarrow H^{k}(\widehat{}\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})
is an isomorphism for
k=0,1
, where both sides are
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
, and is trivial for
k\geq 2
, where
H^{k}(\widehat{}\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})=0
as the cohomological dimension of
\widehat{}\mathbb{Z}
is
1
. So the same holds for each inflation map
\inf:H^{k}(\bar{G}_{n,\sigma},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\rightarrow H^{k}(\bar{G}_{\infty,\sigma},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})
, and the statement follows.
∎

Proof of ii.

Since
H^{k}(\pi_{1}(Q),\mu_{n})=H^{k}(Q,\mu_{n})
, it suffices to prove that the inflation map
H^{k}(G_{n},\mu_{n})\rightarrow H^{k}(\pi_{1}(Q),\mu_{n})
is surjective. Let
E^{p,q}_{r}
and
\tilde{E}^{p,q}_{r}
be the Hochschild–Serre spectral sequences associated to the the pairs
\bar{G}_{n}\triangleleft G_{n}
and
\pi_{1}(\bar{Q})\vartriangleleft\pi_{1}(Q)
, respectively. Their second pages are

E^{p,q}_{2}:=H^{p}(\Gamma_{K},H^{q}(\bar{G}_{n},\mu_{n})),\quad\tilde{E}^{p,q}_{2}:=H^{p}(\Gamma_{K},H^{q}(\pi_{1}(\bar{Q}),\mu_{n}))

and they abut to
H^{k}(G_{n},\mu_{n})
and
H^{k}(\pi_{1}(Q),\mu_{n})
.

Consider, for each pair
p,q
, the composition

H^{p}(\Gamma_{K},H^{q}(\bar{G}_{n},\mu_{n})_{cup})\rightarrow H^{p}(\Gamma_{K},H^{q}(\bar{G}_{n},\mu_{n}))\rightarrow H^{p}(\Gamma_{K},H^{q}(\pi_{1}(\bar{Q}),\mu_{n})),

where the second is the natural map coming from confronting the two sequences.
This composition is an isomorphism by point i. In particular, the first map is injective and the second is surjective. Since
E^{p,q}_{2}=H^{p}(\Gamma_{K},H^{q}(\bar{G}_{n},\mu_{n}))
and
\tilde{E}^{p,q}_{2}=H^{p}(\Gamma_{K},H^{q}(\pi_{1}(\bar{Q}),\mu_{n}))
, this proves that the natural maps
E^{p,q}_{2}\rightarrow\tilde{E}^{p,q}_{2}
are surjective.

Since
\bar{G}_{n}=\pi_{1}(\bar{Q})/n\pi_{1}(\bar{Q})=X_{*}(Q)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})(1)=\Ind^{\Gamma_{E}}_{\Gamma_{K}}((\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})(1))
as
\Gamma_{K}
-modules and
\Ind^{\Gamma_{E}}_{\Gamma_{K}}((\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})(1))\cong\Ind^{\Gamma_{E}}_{\Gamma_{K}}(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})
(as
\mu_{n}\subset E
), the sequence
E^{p,q}_{2}
splits at the second page by Nakaoka’s Theorem
C.1
. Thus, since, as proven above, the maps
E^{p,q}_{2}\rightarrow\tilde{E}^{p,q}_{2}
are surjective for all
p,q
, the spectral sequence
\tilde{E}^{p,q}_{r}
must also degenerate at the second page and then point ii follows from the surjectivities
E^{p,q}_{2}\rightarrow\tilde{E}^{p,q}_{2}
.
∎

Proof of iii.

Nakaoka’s theorem gives a direct sum decomposition
H^{k}(G_{n},\mu_{n})

=\bigoplus_{p+q=k}E^{p,q}_{2}
. The proof above shows that the composition

\bigoplus_{p+q=k}H^{p}(\Gamma_{K},H^{q}(\bar{G}_{n},\mu_{n})_{cup})\rightarrow\bigoplus_{p+q=k}E^{p,q}_{2}=H^{k}(G_{n},\mu_{n})\rightarrow H^{k}(\pi_{1}(\bar{Q}),\mu_{n}),

is surjective (and in fact, even an isomorphism, but we do not need this). Since the restriction
H^{k}(G_{n},\mu_{n})\rightarrow H^{k}(\bar{G}_{n},\mu_{n})
is also expressible as the composition

H^{k}(G_{n},\mu_{n})\rightarrow E^{0,k}_{2}=H^{k}(\bar{G}_{n},\mu_{n})^{\Gamma_{K}}\subset H^{k}(\bar{G}_{n},\mu_{n}),

any element lying in the image of
\bigoplus_{p+q=k}H^{p}(\Gamma_{K},H^{q}(\bar{G}_{n},\mu_{n})_{cup})\rightarrow\bigoplus_{p+q=k}E^{p,q}_{2}
satisfies the sought condition.
∎

Lemma 12.8
.

Let
G
be a profinite group,
H
an open subgroup,
M
a discrete
G
-module, and
k
a natural number. Any element of
Z^{k}(G,M)
that maps to a coborder under the restriction
Z^{k}(G,M)\rightarrow Z^{k}(H,M)
is equivalent up to a coborder to an element that maps to
0
under the same map.

Proof.

Write
\alpha|_{H}=\d(\beta),\,\beta\in C^{k-1}(H,M)
. The restriction
C^{n}(G,M)\rightarrow C^{n}(H,M)
is surjective for all
n
(elements of
C^{n}(H,M)
correspond to continuous functions
H^{n}\rightarrow M
, and each of these clearly extends to continuous functions
G^{n}\rightarrow M
, e.g. by composing with a topological retraction
G\rightarrow H
of
H\rightarrow G
). So we can find
\gamma\in C^{k-1}(G,M)
with
\gamma|_{H}=\beta
, and
\alpha-\d\gamma
maps to
0
in
Z^{k}(H,M)
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
12.4
, modification of
(\alpha,\beta)
to satisfy i-iii.

Taking
k=3
in Lemma
12.7
.ii, the map
H^{3}(G_{n},\mu_{n})\rightarrow\check{H}^{3}(Q^{ur}/Q,\mu_{n})
is surjective. Thus we may immediately modify
\beta
to assume i, and using Lemma
12.7
.iii we also get ii.

To get iii, we view
\beta
as an element of
Z^{3}(G_{n},\mu_{n})
via
\check{Z}^{3}(Q^{\overline{K},n},\mu_{n})=Z^{3}(G_{n},\mu_{n})
(see (
2.3
)). The image of
\beta
under the composition

Z^{3}(G_{n},\mu_{n})\rightarrow H^{3}(G_{n},\mu_{n})\rightarrow H^{3}(\bar{G}_{n},\mu_{n})

maps to
H^{3}(\bar{G}_{n},\mu_{n})_{cup}
. All elements of
H^{3}(\bar{G}_{n},\mu_{n})_{cup}
restrict to zero in
H^{3}(\bar{G}_{n,\text{id}},\mu_{n})
(recall
\bar{G}_{n,\text{id}}=\Gal(Q^{\overline{K},n}/Q^{\overline{K},n,(1)})
), since in each tensor product
H^{1}(\bar{G}_{n,\sigma_{1}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}H^{1}(\bar{G}_{n,\sigma_{2}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}H^{1}(\bar{G}_{n,\sigma_{3}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}),\,\{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}\}\in\binom{\Gamma}{3}
, at least one among
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}
is different from
id
, and the corresponding factor
H^{1}(\bar{G}_{n,\star},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})
maps trivially to
\bar{G}_{n,\text{id}}
, and so thus so does the whole tensor product. Using Lemma
12.8
, we we may therefore assume, after modifying by a coborder, that
\beta
restricts to zero in
Z^{3}(\bar{G}_{n,\text{id}},\mu_{n})={Z}^{3}(\Gal(Q^{\overline{K},n}/Q^{\overline{K},n,(1)}),\mu_{n})
. The subgroups
\Gal(Q^{\overline{K},n}/Q^{L^{\prime},n,(1)})<G_{n},\,\text{ with }L^{\prime}/L
finite form a fundamental system of neighbourhoods of the finite group
\Gal(Q^{\overline{K},n}/Q^{\overline{K},n,(1)})
in
\Gal(Q^{\overline{K},n}/Q)
. Thus, the cocycle
\beta\in Z^{3}(\Gal(Q^{\overline{K},n}/Q),\mu_{n})
, being continuous, restricts trivially to
Z^{3}(\Gal(Q^{\overline{K},n}/Q^{L^{\prime},n,(1)}),\mu_{n})
for some
L^{\prime}
.
∎

12.3.2.
Setup for the triple variation

Since Čech cochains with respect to procoverings are just direct limits of Čech cochains with respect to the underlying inverse systems of coverings, there exists a finite étale connected cover
\tilde{Q}/Q
and a refinement
\varphi:\tilde{\mathcal{V}}\rightarrow\tilde{Q}\times_{Q}X
such that
(\alpha,\beta)
lies in the image of
\check{Z}^{2}(\tilde{\mathcal{V}}/X,\tilde{Q}/Q;\mu_{n})\rightarrow\check{Z}^{2}(\mathcal{V}/X,Q^{ur}/Q;\mu_{n})
with respect to some refinement
(\mathcal{V},Q^{ur},\psi)\rightarrow(\tilde{\mathcal{V}},\tilde{Q},\varphi)
. Since
Q^{ur}=\lim\limits_{\leftarrow,(F,m)}Q^{F,m}
, we may assume that
\tilde{Q}=Q^{F,m}
for some pair
(F,m)
. We may assume
(F,m)\succcurlyeq(L,n)
and that the restriction of
\beta
to
\check{Z}^{3}(\tilde{Q}/Q^{L,n,(1)},\mu_{n})
is zero. We enlarge
S
so that all places of
K
ramifying in
F
or dividing
m
are contained in it, and such that the relative covering
(\tilde{Q}/Q,\mathcal{V}/X,\mathcal{V}\rightarrow\tilde{Q}\times_{Q}X)
spreads out to a relative covering
(\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}/\mathcal{Q},\mathfrak{V}/\mathcal{X},\mathfrak{V}\rightarrow\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}\times_{\mathcal{Q}}\mathcal{X})
of
f:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Q}
, where
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}:=\Spec\mathcal{O}_{F,S}[(y^{\sigma})^{\pm\frac{1}{m}}]_{\sigma\in\Gamma}
.

For
q\in\mathcal{M}
, Lemma
12.2
gives

(12.12)

((P_{v})_{v},q)_{BM}=\sum_{v}\inv_{v}(\alpha(P_{v}(q),\overline{P_{v}(q)})-\gamma(q)_{v}),

for any choice of embeddings
\overline{K}\subset\overline{K}_{v}
, lifts
\bar{q}\in\tilde{Q}(\overline{K}),\,\overline{P_{v}(q)}\in\mathcal{V}(\overline{K}_{v})
satisfying (
12.9
), and any cochain
\gamma(q)\in C^{2}(\overline{K}/K,\mathbb{G}_{m})
such that
\d\gamma(q)=\beta(q,\bar{q})
.
We choose the embeddings and the lifts as in the following proposition, and we choose
\gamma(q)
arbitrarily.

For each archimedean
v
, we endow
\tilde{Q}(\overline{K}_{v})=\bigsqcup_{F\hookrightarrow\overline{K}_{v}}(\overline{K}_{v}^{*})^{\Gamma}
with the diagonal
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-action induced by the inclusions
\mathbb{R}_{>0}\subset\mathbb{C}^{*}\cong\overline{K}_{v}^{*}
.

Proposition 12.9
.

There exist embeddings
\overline{K}\subset\overline{K}_{v}
, lifts
\bar{q},\,\overline{P_{v}(q)}
satisfying (
12.9
), such that:

i.

the point
\bar{q}
lies in
\tilde{Q}(\overline{K})_{incl}\subset\tilde{Q}(\overline{K})
(“incl” stands for the inclusion
F\subset\overline{K}
) for each
q\in\mathcal{M}
;

ii.

(multiplicativity) the function
\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\tilde{Q}(\overline{K})_{incl}:q\mapsto\bar{q}
is multiplicative;

iii.

(locality at non-archimedean places in
S
) for each non-archimedean
v\in S
, the localizations
\bar{q}_{v}:=\bar{q}\otimes_{\overline{K}}\overline{K}_{v}\in\tilde{Q}(\overline{K}_{v}),\,q\in\mathcal{M}
are arbitrarily close to each other;

iv.

(same connected component at archimedean
v
) for each archimedean
v
, the projections
\bar{q}_{v}\cdot\mathbb{R}_{>0}\in\tilde{Q}(\overline{K}_{v})/\mathbb{R}_{>0},\,q\in\mathcal{M}
of the localizations
\bar{q}_{v}\in\tilde{Q}(\overline{K}_{v})
are arbitrarily close to each other;

v.

(compatibility with the multi-section) for every
v\notin S
and every
q\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{Q}(\mathcal{O}_{v})
, the image of
\bar{q}
under the map
\tilde{Q}(\overline{K}_{v})\rightarrow Q^{L,n,(1)}(\overline{K}_{v})
is (the
\overline{K}_{v}
-point associated to)
q^{L,n}
;

vi.

(good reduction) for every
v\notin S
and every
q\in\mathcal{M}
such that
q\in\mathcal{Q}(\mathcal{O}_{v})
, the points
\overline{P_{v}(q)}
lie in the natural image of
\mathfrak{V}(\mathcal{O}_{v}^{ur})
in
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}(\overline{K_{v}})
.

Before proving the proposition, it is helpful to rewrite
\mathcal{M}
as:

\mathcal{M}=q_{b}\cdot\{1,\delta_{1}\}\cdot\{1,\delta_{2}\}\cdot\{1,\delta_{3}\},\,q_{b}:=q_{0}q_{1,1}q_{2,1}q_{3,1},\,\delta_{i}:=q_{i,2}q_{i,1}^{-1}.

For
v\notin S
such that
\mathcal{M}\not\subset\mathcal{Q}(\mathcal{O}_{v})
, we also rewrite the intersection
\mathcal{M}_{v}:=\mathcal{M}\cap\mathcal{Q}(\mathcal{O}_{v})
as in the proof of Lemma
11.1
:

\mathcal{M}_{v}=\begin{cases}\mathcal{M}&\text{ if }i_{v}=0;\\
q_{\text{base}(v)}\cdot\prod_{i\neq i_{v}}\{1,\delta_{i}\}&\text{ if }i_{v}\neq 0;\end{cases}

where
i_{v}:=0
if
v(q_{0})>0
, otherwise
i_{v}
is defined as the first coordinate of the pair
(i_{v},j_{v})
for which the associated
q_{i_{v},j_{v}}
corresponds to the prime ideal associated to
v
, and
q_{\text{base}(v)}:=q_{0}\cdot q_{i_{v},j_{v}}\cdot\prod_{i\neq i_{v}}q_{i_{v},1}
.

We need the following two lemmas:

Lemma 12.10
.

Let
\Omega
be a field,
\Omega^{sep}
be a separable closure of
\Omega
, and
\tilde{\Omega}
be a second separably closed field containing
\Omega
. Let
m
be a natural number, and
D=\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{d}\}\subset\Omega^{*}
be a subset such that the algebra
\Omega[\sqrt[m]{y}]_{y\in D}:=\Omega[t_{1},\ldots,t_{d}]/(t_{i}^{m}-y_{i})_{i=1,\ldots,d}
is a field. Let
\sqrt[m]{y_{1}},\ldots,\sqrt[m]{y_{d}}
be
m
-th roots of
y_{1},\ldots,y_{d}
in
\Omega^{sep}
. Then for every choice of
m
-th roots
\widetilde{\sqrt[m]{y_{1}}},\ldots,\widetilde{\sqrt[m]{y_{d}}}
of
y_{1},\ldots,y_{d}
in
\tilde{\Omega}
, there exists an
\Omega
-embedding
\Omega^{sep}\subset\tilde{\Omega}
sending each
\sqrt[m]{y_{i}}
to
\widetilde{\sqrt[m]{y_{i}}}
.

Proof.

The roots
\{\sqrt[m]{y}\}_{y\in D}
and
\{\widetilde{\sqrt[m]{y}}\}_{y\in D}
determine homomorphisms of
\Omega[\sqrt[m]{y}]_{y\in D}
with values in
\Omega^{sep}
and
\tilde{\Omega}
, respectively. Since
\Omega[\sqrt[m]{y}]_{y\in D}
is a field, both homomorphisms are embeddings. Extending then the identity on
\Omega[\sqrt[m]{y}]_{y\in D}
to a field embedding
\Omega^{sep}\subset\tilde{\Omega}
gives the desired embedding.
∎

Lemma 12.11
.

The algebra
L_{A}:=L[\sqrt[m]{y}]_{y\in A},\quad A:=\{q_{b}^{\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\Gamma}\cup\{\delta_{1}\}_{\sigma\in\Gamma}\cup\{\delta_{2}\}_{\sigma\in\Gamma}\cup\{\delta_{3}\}_{\sigma\in\Gamma}
is a field.

Proof.

We may embed
L_{A}
in

L_{B}:=L[\sqrt[m]{y}]_{y\in B},\quad B:=\{q_{i,j}^{\sigma}\}_{{\sigma\in\Gamma,\,i\in\{1,2,3\},\,j\in\{1,2\}}},

via
\sqrt[m]{q_{b}^{\sigma}}\mapsto\sqrt[m]{q_{1,1}^{\sigma}}\sqrt[m]{q_{2,1}^{\sigma}}\sqrt[m]{q_{3,1}^{\sigma}},\,\sqrt[m]{\delta_{i}^{\sigma}}\mapsto\sqrt[m]{q_{i,2}^{\sigma}}\sqrt[m]{q_{i,1}^{\sigma}}
. Note that
L_{B}
is a field. In fact, we have
L_{B}=L\otimes_{E}E[\sqrt[m]{y}]_{y\in B}
and the fields
L,\,E[\sqrt[m]{y}],\,y\in B
are linearly disjoint over
E
, since for each
y
the field
E[\sqrt[m]{y}]
is totally ramified over
w_{y}
(as
w_{y}(y)=1
), and unramified over
w_{y^{\prime}}
for
y^{\prime}\neq y
, and
L
is unramified over each
w_{y}
. Thus
L_{A}
is a field as well as wished.
∎

Proof of Proposition
12.9
.

Via the identification
\tilde{Q}(\overline{K})_{incl}=(\overline{K}^{*})^{\Gamma}
, lifts
\bar{q}\in\tilde{Q}(\overline{K})_{incl}
of a
q\in Q(K)=E^{*}
correspond to vectors
(\sqrt[m]{q^{\sigma}})_{\sigma\in\Gamma}
of
m
-th roots of the conjugates of
q
.

Choose arbitrary
m
-th roots in
\overline{K}
of all elements of
A
. Such choice of roots determines by multiplicative extension a choice of
m
-th roots
\sqrt[m]{q^{\sigma}}
for all
q\in\mathcal{M}
and
\sigma\in\Gamma
. These determine lifts
\bar{q}\in\tilde{Q}(\overline{K})_{incl}
that are multiplicative by construction, giving i and ii.

We shall get points iii-v by appropriately choosing the embeddings
\overline{K}\subset\overline{K}_{v}
with the help of the lemmas above.

For non-archimedean
v\in S
, we have that each
\delta_{i}
is arbitrarily close to
1\in Q(K_{v})
, so the vector
(\delta_{i}^{\sigma})_{\sigma\in\Gamma}\in E^{\Gamma}
is
w
-adically arbitrarily close to
(1,\ldots,1)
for any place
w
of
E
dividing
v
. Choosing one
w
and local
w
-adic roots in
\overline{E}_{w}=\overline{K}_{v}
that are all close to
1
, we may apply Lemma
12.10
with
E=\Omega
to infer that there exists an
E
-embedding
\overline{K}\subset\overline{K}_{v}
under which all global
m
-th roots of the conjugates of the
\delta_{i}
map to the just determined local roots that are close to
1
. This gives iii.

For archimedean
v
, we argue analogously after scaling. More precisely, there exists, for each
i
, an element
t_{i}\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
such that
\delta_{i}\cdot t_{i}^{-1}
is arbitrarily close to
1\in Q(K_{v})
. So, for any place
w
of
E
dividing
v
, the vector
(\delta_{i}^{\sigma}t_{i}^{-1})_{\sigma\in\Gamma}\in E_{w}^{\Gamma}
is arbitrarily close to
(1,\ldots,1)
. Arguing as for the non-archimedean case, we find an embedding
\overline{K}\subset\overline{K}_{v}
under which each global root
\sqrt[m]{\delta_{i}^{\sigma}}
maps to the local root of
\delta_{i}^{\sigma}t_{i}^{-1}
close to
1
times
\sqrt[m]{t_{i}}
. This gives iv.

Finally, let
v\notin S
be such that
\mathcal{M}\cap\mathcal{Q}(\mathcal{O}_{v})\neq\emptyset
, and
\iota
be the embedding
L\subset K_{v}
determined by the lifts
q^{L,n}
as in Lemma
11.1
. The lifts
q^{L,n}
determine
n
-th roots
\sqrt[n]{q^{\sigma}}
in
K_{v}
for all
q\in\mathcal{M}_{v},\,\sigma\in\Gamma^{*}
that are multiplicative in the variable
q\in\mathcal{M}_{v}
. Applying Lemma
12.10
with
L=\Omega
, we find an embedding
\overline{K}\subset\overline{K}_{v}
extending the embedding
\iota
under which each global root

\sqrt[n]{q_{\text{base}(v)}^{\sigma}}:=\left(\sqrt[m]{q_{\text{base}(v)}^{\sigma}}\right)^{m/n},\,\sqrt[n]{\delta_{i}^{\sigma}}:=(\sqrt[m]{\delta_{i}^{\sigma}})^{m/n},\,\sigma\in\Gamma^{*},i\neq i_{v}

determined above maps to the corresponding local root in
\overline{K}_{v}
. This gives v.

For
v\notin S
such that
\mathcal{M}\cap\mathcal{Q}(\mathcal{O}_{v})=\emptyset
, we choose
\overline{K}\subset\overline{K}_{v}
arbitrarily.

For each
q\in\mathcal{M}
and all
v\in S
or
v
such that
q\notin\mathcal{Q}(\mathcal{O}_{v})
, we choose any lift
\overline{P_{v}(q)}\in\mathcal{V}(\overline{K}_{v})
as in Remark
12.3
(2), guaranteeing the compatibility (
12.9
).

Finally, for
v\notin S
and
q\in\mathcal{M}\cap\mathcal{Q}(\mathcal{O}_{v})
, it remains to create the lifts
\overline{P_{v}(q)}
in such a way that vi holds. Since
q\in\mathcal{Q}(\mathcal{O}_{v})
and
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}\rightarrow\mathcal{Q}
is finite étale, the localization
\bar{q}_{v}:\Spec\overline{K}_{v}\rightarrow\tilde{Q}
extends to a morphism
\Spec\overline{\mathcal{O}_{v}}\rightarrow\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}
which arises from a morphism
\Spec\mathcal{O}_{v}^{ur}\rightarrow\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}
. We still denote the latter by
\bar{q}_{v}
with a slight abuse of notation. Consider now the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec\mathcal{O}_{v}^{ur}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec\mathcal{O}_{v}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{Q}}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(P_{v}(q),\bar{q}_{v})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{v}(q)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}

The dotted lift may be found by the Henselianity of
\Spec\mathcal{O}_{v}^{ur}
and the surjectivity of
\psi
. Choosing as lift
\overline{P_{v}(q)}:\Spec\overline{K}_{v}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
the one induced by the map
\Spec\mathcal{O}_{v}^{ur}\rightarrow\mathfrak{V}
above, both vi and the compatibility (
12.9
) hold.
∎

To simplify notation, we let
\Delta^{(3)}:=\sum_{q\in\mathcal{M}}(-1)^{\sgn(q)}
. Applying
\Delta^{(3)}
to (
12.12
), we get

(12.13)

\Delta^{(3)}((P_{v})_{v},q)_{BM}=\sum_{v}\inv_{v}\left(\Delta^{(3)}\alpha(P_{v}(q),\overline{P_{v}(q)})-(\Delta^{(3)}\gamma(q))_{v}\right).

We have
\d(\Delta^{(3)}\gamma(q))=\Delta^{(3)}\beta(q,\bar{q})
. Let us prove that this is a completely unramified cochain (in the sense of Section
5
). In fact, for
v\in S
, the cocycle
\beta(q,\bar{q})_{v}\in\check{Z}^{3}(\overline{K}_{v}/K_{v},\mu_{n})
is constant throughout
\mathcal{M}
by our locality assumption since
\check{Z}^{3}(\overline{K}_{v}/K_{v},\mu_{n})=Z^{3}(\Gamma_{v},\mu_{n})
is discrete as a topological space. So its triple variation vanishes. For
v\notin S
and
q\in\mathcal{Q}(\mathcal{O}_{v})
, the cocycle
\beta(q,\bar{q})_{v}
is unramified if
q\in\mathcal{Q}(\mathcal{O}_{v})
by our good reduction assumption. For
v\notin S
and
q\notin\mathcal{Q}(\mathcal{O}_{v})
, we have
\beta(q,\bar{q})_{v}=0
as
\beta(q,\bar{q})_{v}
is the pullback of
\beta
along the composition

{(\Spec K_{v},\Spec\overline{K}_{v})}\rightarrow{(\Spec K,\Spec\overline{K})}\xrightarrow{(q,\bar{q})}{(Q,\tilde{Q})}

which, by our assumption of compatibility with the multi-section, coincides with the composition

(\Spec K_{v},\Spec\overline{K}_{v})\xrightarrow{(q^{L,n},\bar{q}_{v})}(Q^{L,n,(1)},\tilde{Q})\rightarrow{(Q,\tilde{Q})},

and in
12.3.1
we chose
\beta
so that its restriction to
(Q^{L,n,(1)},\tilde{Q})
is trivial.

Since
\d(\Delta^{(3)}\gamma(q))
is completely unramified, we may split (
12.13
) as:

\Delta^{(3)}((P_{v})_{v},q)_{BM}\\
=\sum_{v}\inv_{v}\left(\Delta^{(3)}\alpha(P_{v}(q),\overline{P_{v}(q)})-\eta_{v}\right)-\sum_{v}\inv_{v}\left((\Delta^{(3)}\gamma(q))_{v}-\eta_{v}\right),

where
\eta_{v}
is any unramified primitive of
\Delta^{(3)}\beta(q,\bar{q})_{v}
. We treat the two contributions separately.

12.3.3.
First contribution

We need the following two auxiliary lemmas.

Lemma 12.12
.

Let
S
be a scheme, and

\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{\overset{\psi_{1}}{\underset{\psi_{2}}{\rightrightarrows}}}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\psi_{0}}\mathcal{U}

be a commutative diagram of procoverings of
S
. Let
P
be an étale sheaf on
S
, and
\alpha\in\check{C}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}/S,P)
be such that
\d\alpha=\psi_{0}^{*}\beta
for some
\beta\in\check{Z}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{U}}/S,P)
. Then the two pullbacks
\psi_{1}^{*}\alpha
and
\psi_{2}^{*}\alpha
differ by a coborder.

Proof.

We have
\psi_{1}^{*}\alpha-\psi_{2}^{*}\alpha=\d(K_{\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}(\alpha))+K_{\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}(\d\alpha)
with the homotopy
K_{\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}:\check{C}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}/S,P)\rightarrow\check{C}^{\bullet-1}({\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}}/S,P)
defined in (
2.1
).
Moreover, writing
\d\alpha=\psi_{0}^{*}\beta,\,\beta\in C^{\bullet+1}(\mathcal{U},P)
, we have

K_{\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}(\d\alpha)=K_{\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}(\psi_{0}^{*}(\beta))=K_{\psi_{0}\circ\psi_{1},\psi_{0}\circ\psi_{2}}(\beta)=\d K^{(2)}_{\psi_{0}\circ\psi_{1}}(\beta),

where we used
\psi_{0}\circ\psi_{1}=\psi_{0}\circ\psi_{2}
in the end, and
K^{(2)}:\check{C}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{U}}/S,P)\rightarrow\check{C}^{\bullet-2}({\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}}/S,P)
denotes the
2
-homotopy (
2.2
) (the
2
-homotopy described therein applies to a refinement of coverings, but taking a direct limit we immediately get an analogous homotopy for a refinement of procoverings).
∎

Lemma 12.13
.

Let
S
be a scheme,
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\psi}\mathcal{U}
be a refinement of coverings, and
P
be an étale sheaf on
S
. Let
\alpha\in\check{C}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}/S,P)
be such that
\d\alpha=\psi^{*}\beta
with
\beta\in\check{Z}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{U}}/S,P)
. Let
\Omega
be a field with separable closure
\Omega^{sep}
. Let
P\in S(\Omega)
, and
\bar{P}_{1},\bar{P}_{2}\in\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(\Omega^{sep})
be two lifts of
P
with the same projection in
\mathcal{U}(\Omega^{sep})
. Then
\alpha(P,\bar{P}_{1})-\alpha(P,\bar{P}_{2})
is a coborder.

Proof.

We may assume
S=P
. Then this reduces to the previous lemma by taking
\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}=\Spec\Omega^{sep}
.
∎

We shall show that
\inv_{v}\left(\Delta^{(3)}\alpha(P_{v}(q),\overline{P_{v}(q)})-\eta_{v}\right)=0
for all
v
.
We start by proving that
\Delta^{(3)}\alpha(P_{v}(q),\overline{P_{v}(q)})
is a coborder for
v\in S
. Choosing
\eta_{v}=0
, this gives the sought vanishing for
v\in S
.
Define

\Xi_{v}:=X(K_{v})\times_{X(\overline{K}_{v})}\tilde{\mathcal{V}}(\overline{K}_{v}),\quad\Xi_{v}^{0}:=X(K_{v})\times_{Q(\overline{K}_{v})}\tilde{Q}(\overline{K}_{v}),

which we view as topological spaces through the
v
-adic topology. The natural map
\Xi_{v}\rightarrow\Xi_{v}^{0}
is continuous and open as it is locally defined by specializing étale maps on
K_{v}
-points, and it is also surjective by the surjectivity of
\psi
. Let
C^{2}:=\check{C}^{2}(\overline{K}_{v}/K_{v},\mu_{n})
, which we endow with the discrete topology, and
B^{2}:=\check{B}^{2}(\overline{K}_{v}/K_{v},\mu_{n})
. By Lemma
12.13
, the function
\Xi_{v}\rightarrow C^{2},\,(P,\bar{P})\mapsto\alpha(P,\bar{P})
sits in a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Xi_{v}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Xi_{v}^{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{2}/B^{2}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha\mapsto\alpha(P,\bar{P})}

Since
\Xi_{v}\rightarrow C^{2}
is clearly continuous and
\Xi_{v}\rightarrow\Xi^{0}_{v}
is open and surjective, the induced map
\Xi_{v}^{0}\rightarrow C^{2}/B^{2}
is also continuous (by the diagram, the inverse image of an open under this last map is the projection of an open under
\Xi_{v}\rightarrow\Xi^{0}_{v}
).

The image of a pair
(P_{v}(q),\overline{P_{v}(q)})\in\Xi_{v}
in
\Xi^{0}_{v}
is
(P_{v}(q),\bar{q}_{v})
.

For non-archimedean
v\in S
, the pairs
(P_{v}(q),\bar{q}_{v})\in\Xi^{0}_{v},\,q\in\mathcal{M}
are arbitrarily close to each other by condition iii.

On the other hand, for archimedean
v
, the pairs
(P_{v}(q),\bar{q}_{v})\in\Xi^{0}_{v},\,q\in\mathcal{M}
all lie on the same connected component of
\Xi^{0}_{v}
. In fact, by condition iv, the localizations
\bar{q}_{v}
all lie on the same connected component
\tilde{V}_{v}\subset\tilde{Q}(\overline{K}_{v})
of the inverse image of
V_{v}
under
\tilde{Q}(\overline{K}_{v})\rightarrow Q(\overline{K}_{v})
. The pairs
(P_{v}(q),\bar{q}_{v})
then all lie in

\tilde{U}_{v}:=U_{v}\times_{V_{v}}\tilde{V}_{v}\subset X(K_{v})\times_{Q(\overline{K}_{v})}\tilde{Q}(\overline{K}_{v})=\Xi^{0}_{v}.

The projection
\tilde{U}_{v}\rightarrow\tilde{V}_{v}
has connected fibers as it is a base-change of
U_{v}\rightarrow V_{v}
, which has connected fibers by hypothesis. Thus, since
\tilde{V}_{v}
is connected,
\tilde{U}_{v}
is connected as well.

In both the archimedean and the non-archimedean case, the function
\mathcal{M}\rightarrow C^{2}/B^{2},q\mapsto\alpha(q,\bar{q})
is constant. In particular, its triple variation vanishes. I.e.
\Delta^{(3)}\alpha(P_{v}(q),\overline{P_{v}(q)})
is a coborder for
v\in S
, as wished.

Let now
v\notin S
. We may assume
\mathcal{M}\cap\mathcal{Q}(\mathcal{O}_{v})\neq\emptyset
, for otherwise the cochain
\Delta^{(3)}\alpha(P_{v}(q),\overline{P_{v}(q)})
is unramified, and so
\inv_{v}\left(\Delta^{(3)}\alpha(P_{v}(q),\overline{P_{v}(q)})-\eta_{v}\right)=0
as this is the local invariant of an unramified cocycle.

For
q\in\mathcal{M}_{v}=\mathcal{M}\cap\mathcal{Q}(\mathcal{O}_{v})
, we have
\beta(q,\bar{q})_{v}=0
, and so
\alpha(P_{v}(q),\overline{P_{v}(q)})
is a cocycle.
Let
Q_{sec}:=Q^{L,n,(1)}
. This maps to
X
via
s:Q_{sec}\rightarrow X
. Pulling back
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}
along
s
, we obtain a covering
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}\times_{X}Q_{sec}
of
Q_{sec}
. We let
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{sec}\subset\tilde{\mathcal{V}}\times_{X}Q_{sec}
be defined by the cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{sec}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}\times_{X}Q_{sec}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{Q}\times_{Q}Q_{sec},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\psi,\text{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}

where
\Delta:\tilde{Q}\hookrightarrow\tilde{Q}\times_{Q}Q_{sec}
is the “diagonal map”, i.e. the map that is the identity on the first coordinate and the natural projection
\tilde{Q}\rightarrow Q_{sec}
on the second. This is an open-closed embedding, and thus
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{sec}\rightarrow\tilde{\mathcal{V}}\times_{X}Q_{sec}
is as well. Thus the projection
\mathcal{V}_{sec}\rightarrow\tilde{Q}
is a collection of étale maps. Moreover, the surjectivity of
\psi
implies the surjectivity of
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{sec}\rightarrow\tilde{Q}
. In particular,
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{sec}
is a covering of
Q_{sec}
.

Now the definition of
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{sec}
gives the commutative diagram of procovered schemes in display on the left, which thus induces by pullback a commutative diagram on Čech cochains on the right:

\hbox to158.24pt{\vbox to54.02pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 79.11665pt\lower-27.00893pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

{
